| [[characters/achilles\|ACHILLES]] | Who’s there? |
| PATROCLUS | [[characters/thersites\|Thersites]], my lord. |
| [[characters/achilles\|ACHILLES]] | Where? Where? O where?—Art thou come? Why, my cheese, my digestion, why hast thou not served thyself into my table so many meals? Come: what’s [[characters/agamemnon\|Agamemnon]]? |
| [[characters/thersites\|THERSITES]] | Thy commander, [[characters/achilles\|Achilles]].—Then tell me, Patroclus, what’s [[characters/achilles\|Achilles]]? |
| PATROCLUS | Thy lord, [[characters/thersites\|Thersites]]. Then tell me, I pray thee, what’s [[characters/thersites\|Thersites]]? |
| [[characters/thersites\|THERSITES]] | Thy knower, Patroclus. Then tell me, Patroclus, what art thou? |
| PATROCLUS | Thou mayst tell, that knowest. |
| [[characters/achilles\|ACHILLES]] | O tell, tell. |
| [[characters/thersites\|THERSITES]] | I’ll decline the whole question. [[characters/agamemnon\|Agamemnon]] commands [[characters/achilles\|Achilles]], [[characters/achilles\|Achilles]] is my lord, I am Patroclus’ knower, and Patroclus is a [[archetypes/fool\|fool]]. |
| PATROCLUS | You rascal. |
| [[characters/thersites\|THERSITES]] | Peace, [[archetypes/fool\|fool]], I have not done. |
| [[characters/achilles\|ACHILLES]] | (to Patroclus) He is a privileged man.—Proceed, [[characters/thersites\|Thersites]]. |
| [[characters/thersites\|THERSITES]] | [[characters/agamemnon\|Agamemnon]] is a [[archetypes/fool\|fool]], [[characters/achilles\|Achilles]] is a [[archetypes/fool\|fool]], [[characters/thersites\|Thersites]] is a [[archetypes/fool\|fool]], and as aforesaid Patroclus is a [[archetypes/fool\|fool]]. |
| [[characters/achilles\|ACHILLES]] | Derive this. Come. |
| [[characters/thersites\|THERSITES]] | [[characters/agamemnon\|Agamemnon]] is a [[archetypes/fool\|fool]] to offer to command [[characters/achilles\|Achilles]]; [[characters/achilles\|Achilles]] is a [[archetypes/fool\|fool]] to be commanded of [[characters/agamemnon\|Agamemnon]]; [[characters/thersites\|Thersites]] is a [[archetypes/fool\|fool]] to serve such a [[archetypes/fool\|fool]]; and Patroclus is a [[archetypes/fool\|fool]] positive. |
| PATROCLUS | Why am I a [[archetypes/fool\|fool]]? |
| [[characters/thersites\|THERSITES]] | Make that demand to the Creator. It suffices me thou art. Look you, who comes here? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/agamemnon\|Agamemnon]], [[characters/ulysses\|Ulysses]], Nestor, Diomedes, [[characters/ajax\|Ajax]], and Calchas |
| [[characters/achilles\|ACHILLES]] | Patroclus, I’ll speak with nobody.—Come in with me, [[characters/thersites\|Thersites]]. Exit |
| [[characters/thersites\|THERSITES]] | Here is such patchery, such juggling and such knavery. All the argument is a whore and a cuckold. A good quarrel to draw emulous factions and bleed to [[motifs/death\|death]] upon. Now the dry serpigo on the subject, and war and lechery confound all. Exit |
| [[characters/agamemnon\|AGAMEMNON]] | (to Patroclus) Where is [[characters/achilles\|Achilles]]? |
| *(didascalia)* | Within his tent; but ill-disposed, my lord. |
| [[characters/agamemnon\|AGAMEMNON]] | Let it be known to him that we are here.<br>He faced our messengers, and we lay by<br>Our appertainments, visiting of him.<br>Let him be told so, lest perchance he think<br>We dare not move the question of our place,<br>Or know not what we are. |
| PATROCLUS | I shall so say to him. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| [[characters/ulysses\|ULYSSES]] | We saw him at the opening of his tent.<br>He is not sick. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/ajax\|AJAX]] Yes, [[motifs/lion\|lion]]-sick: sick of proud heart. You may call it ‘[[concepts/melancholy\|melancholy]]’ if you will favour the man, but by my head ’tis [[concepts/pride\|pride]]. But why? Why? Let him show us the cause. To [[characters/agamemnon\|Agamemnon]] A word, my lord. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/ajax\|Ajax]] and [[characters/agamemnon\|Agamemnon]] talk apart |
| NESTOR | What moves [[characters/ajax\|Ajax]] thus to bay at him? |
| [[characters/ulysses\|ULYSSES]] | [[characters/achilles\|Achilles]] hath inveigled his [[archetypes/fool\|fool]] from him. |
| NESTOR | Who? [[characters/thersites\|Thersites]]? |
| [[characters/ulysses\|ULYSSES]] | He. |
| NESTOR | Then will [[characters/ajax\|Ajax]] lack matter, if he have lost his argument. |
| [[characters/ulysses\|ULYSSES]] | No, you see, he is his argument that has his argument: [[characters/achilles\|Achilles]]. |
| NESTOR | All the better—their fraction is more our wish than their faction. But it was a strong council that a [[archetypes/fool\|fool]] could disunite. |
| [[characters/ulysses\|ULYSSES]] | The amity that wisdom knits not, folly may easily untie. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Patroclus |
| [[characters/ulysses\|ULYSSES]] | Here comes Patroclus. |
| NESTOR | No [[characters/achilles\|Achilles]] with him. |
| [[characters/ulysses\|ULYSSES]] | The elephant hath joints, but none for courtesy: his legs are legs for necessity, not for flexure. |
| PATROCLUS | (to [[characters/agamemnon\|Agamemnon]])<br>[[characters/achilles\|Achilles]] bids me say he is much sorry<br>If anything more than your sport and pleasure<br>Did move your [[concepts/greatness\|greatness]] and this noble state<br>To call upon him. He hopes it is no other<br>But for your health and your digestion’s sake: no<br>An after-dinner’s breath. |
| [[characters/agamemnon\|AGAMEMNON]] | Hear you, Patroclus.<br>We are too [[motifs/well\|well]] acquainted with these answers.<br>But his evasion, winged thus swift with scorn,<br>Cannot outfly our apprehensions.<br>Much attribute he hath, and much the reason<br>Why we ascribe it to him. Yet all his virtues,<br>Not virtuously on his own part beheld,<br>Do in our eyes begin to lose their gloss,<br>Yea, and like fair fruit in an unwholesome dish<br>Are like to rot untasted. Go and tell him<br>We come to speak with him—and you shall not sin<br>If you do say we think him over-proud<br>And under-honest, in [[concepts/self\|self]]-assumption greater<br>Than in the note of judgement. And worthier than<br>himself<br>Here tend the savage strangeness he puts on,<br>Disguise the holy strength of their command,<br>And underwrite in an observing kind<br>His humorous predominance—yea, watch<br>His pettish lunes, his ebbs, his flows, as if<br>The passage and whole carriage of this action<br>Rode on his tide. Go tell him this, and add<br>That if he overhold his price so much<br>We’ll none of him, but let him, like an engine<br>Not portable, lie under this report:<br>‘Bring action hither, this cannot go to war.’<br>A stirring [[characters/dwarf\|dwarf]] we do allowance give<br>Before a sleeping giant. Tell him so. |
| PATROCLUS | I shall, and bring his answer presently. |
| [[characters/agamemnon\|AGAMEMNON]] | In second voice we’ll not be satisfied;<br>We come to speak with him.—[[characters/ulysses\|Ulysses]], enter you. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[characters/ulysses\|Ulysses]] with Patroclus |
| [[characters/ajax\|AJAX]] | What is he more than another? |
| [[characters/agamemnon\|AGAMEMNON]] | No more than what he thinks he is. |
| [[characters/ajax\|AJAX]] | Is he so much? Do you not think he thinks himself a better man than I am? |
| [[characters/agamemnon\|AGAMEMNON]] | No question. |
| [[characters/ajax\|AJAX]] | Will you subscribe his thought, and say he is? |
| [[characters/agamemnon\|AGAMEMNON]] | No, noble [[characters/ajax\|Ajax]]. You are as strong, as valiant, as wise, no less noble, much more gentle, and altogether more tractable. |
| [[characters/ajax\|AJAX]] | Why should a man be proud? How doth [[concepts/pride\|pride]] grow? I know not what it is. |
| [[characters/agamemnon\|AGAMEMNON]] | Your mind is the clearer, [[characters/ajax\|Ajax]], and your virtues the fairer. He that is proud eats up himself. [[concepts/pride\|Pride]] is his own glass, his own trumpet, his own chronicle—and whatever praises itself but in the deed devours the deed in the praise. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/ulysses\|Ulysses]] |
| [[characters/ajax\|AJAX]] | I do hate a proud man as I hate the engendering of toads. |
| NESTOR | (aside) Yet he loves himself. Is’t not strange? |
| [[characters/ulysses\|ULYSSES]] | [[characters/achilles\|Achilles]] will not to the field tomorrow. |
| [[characters/agamemnon\|AGAMEMNON]] | What’s his excuse? |
| [[characters/ulysses\|ULYSSES]] | He doth rely on none,<br>But carries on the stream of his dispose<br>Without observance or respect of any,<br>In will peculiar and in [[concepts/self\|self]]-admission. |
| [[characters/agamemnon\|AGAMEMNON]] | Why, will he not, upon our fair request,<br>Untent his person and share the air with us? |
| [[characters/ulysses\|ULYSSES]] | Things small as nothing, for request’s sake only,<br>He makes important. Possessed he is with [[concepts/greatness\|greatness]],<br>And speaks not to himself but with a [[concepts/pride\|pride]]<br>That quarrels at [[concepts/self\|self]]-breath. Imagined worth<br>Holds in his [[motifs/blood\|blood]] such swoll’n and hot discourse<br>That ‘twixt his mental and his active parts<br>Kingdomed [[characters/achilles\|Achilles]] in commotion rages<br>And batters ’gainst himself. What should I say?<br>He is so plaguy proud that the [[motifs/death\|death]] tokens of it<br>Cry ‘No recovery’. |
| [[characters/agamemnon\|AGAMEMNON]] | Let [[characters/ajax\|Ajax]] go to him.<br>(To [[characters/ajax\|Ajax]]) Dear lord, go you and greet him in his tent.<br>’Tis said he holds you [[motifs/well\|well]] and will be led,<br>At your request, a little from himself. |
| [[characters/ulysses\|ULYSSES]] | O [[characters/agamemnon\|Agamemnon]], let it not be so.<br>We’ll consecrate the steps that [[characters/ajax\|Ajax]] makes<br>When they go from [[characters/achilles\|Achilles]]. Shall the proud lord<br>That bastes his arrogance with his own seam<br>And never suffers matter of the world |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter his thoughts, save such as do revolve |
| [[characters/ulysses\|ULYSSES]] | And ruminate himself—shall he be worshipped<br>Of that we hold an idol more than he?<br>No, this thrice-worthy and right valiant lord<br>Must not so stale his palm, nobly acquired,<br>Nor by my will assubjugate his merit,<br>As amply titled as [[characters/achilles\|Achilles]]’ is,<br>By going to [[characters/achilles\|Achilles]]—<br>That were to enlard his fat-already [[concepts/pride\|pride]]<br>And add more coals to Cancer when he burns<br>With entertaining great [[characters/hyperion\|Hyperion]].<br>This lord go to him? Jupiter forbid,<br>And say in thunder ‘[[characters/achilles\|Achilles]], go to him’. |
| NESTOR | (aside to Diomedes)<br>O this is [[motifs/well\|well]]. He rubs the vein of him. |
| DIOMEDES | (aside to Nestor)<br>And how his [[motifs/silence\|silence]] drinks up this applause. |
| [[characters/ajax\|AJAX]] | If I go to him, with my armed fist<br>I’ll pash him o’er the face. |
| [[characters/agamemnon\|AGAMEMNON]] | O no, you shall not go. |
| [[characters/ajax\|AJAX]] | An a be proud with me, I’ll feeze his [[concepts/pride\|pride]].<br>Let me go to him. |
| [[characters/ulysses\|ULYSSES]] | Not for the worth that hangs upon our quarrel. |
| [[characters/ajax\|AJAX]] | A paltry insolent fellow. |
| NESTOR | (aside) How he describes himself! |
| [[characters/ajax\|AJAX]] | Can he not be sociable? |
| [[characters/ulysses\|ULYSSES]] | (aside) The raven chides blackness. |
| [[characters/ajax\|AJAX]] | I’ll let his humour’s [[motifs/blood\|blood]]. |
| [[characters/agamemnon\|AGAMEMNON]] | (aside) He will be the physician that should be the patient. |
| [[characters/ajax\|AJAX]] | An all men were o’ my mind— |
| [[characters/ulysses\|ULYSSES]] | (aside) Wit would be out of fashion. |
| [[characters/ajax\|AJAX]] | A should not bear it so. A should eat swords first.<br>Shall [[concepts/pride\|pride]] carry it? |
| NESTOR | (aside) An’t would, you’d carry half. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/ajax\|AJAX]] A would have ten shares. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/ulysses\|ULYSSES]] (aside) I will knead him; I’ll make him supple. |
| NESTOR | He’s not yet through warm. |
| NESTOR | (aside) Farce him with praises. Pour in, pour in!<br>His [[concepts/ambition\|ambition]] is dry. |
| [[characters/ulysses\|ULYSSES]] | (to [[characters/agamemnon\|Agamemnon]])<br>My lord, you feed too much on this dislike. |
| NESTOR | (to [[characters/agamemnon\|Agamemnon]])<br>Our noble general, do not do so. |
| DIOMEDES | (to [[characters/agamemnon\|Agamemnon]])<br>You must prepare to fight without [[characters/achilles\|Achilles]]. |
| [[characters/ulysses\|ULYSSES]] | Why, ‘tis this naming of him does him harm.<br>Here is a man—but ’tis before his face.<br>I will be silent. |
| NESTOR | Wherefore should you so?<br>He is not emulous, as [[characters/achilles\|Achilles]] is. |
| [[characters/ulysses\|ULYSSES]] | Know the whole world he is as valiant— |
| [[characters/ajax\|AJAX]] | A whoreson dog, that shall palter thus with us—would he were a Trojan! |
| NESTOR | What a vice were it in [[characters/ajax\|Ajax]] now— |
| [[characters/ulysses\|ULYSSES]] | If he were proud— |
| DIOMEDES | Or covetous of praise— |
| [[characters/ulysses\|ULYSSES]] | Ay, or surly borne— |
| DIOMEDES | Or strange, or [[concepts/self\|self]]-affected. |
| [[characters/ulysses\|ULYSSES]] | (to [[characters/ajax\|Ajax]])<br>Thank the heavens, lord, thou art of sweet composure.<br>Praise him that got thee, she that gave thee suck.<br>Famed be thy tutor, and thy parts of [[concepts/nature\|nature]]<br>Thrice famed beyond, beyond all erudition.<br>But he that disciplined thine arms to fight—<br>Let Mars divide [[concepts/eternity\|eternity]] in twain,<br>And give him half. And for thy vigour,<br>Bull-bearing Milo his addition yield<br>To sinewy [[characters/ajax\|Ajax]]. I will not praise thy wisdom,<br>Which like a bourn, a pale, a shore confines<br>Thy spacious and dilated parts. Here’s Nestor,<br>Instructed by the antiquary times:<br>He must, he is, he cannot but be, wise.<br>But pardon, father Nestor: were your days<br>As green as [[characters/ajax\|Ajax]]’, and your brain so tempered,<br>You should not have the eminence of him,<br>But be as [[characters/ajax\|Ajax]]. |
| [[characters/ajax\|AJAX]] | Shall I call you father? |
| [[characters/ulysses\|ULYSSES]] | Ay, my good son. |
| DIOMEDES | Be ruled by him, Lord [[characters/ajax\|Ajax]]. |
| [[characters/ulysses\|ULYSSES]] | (to [[characters/agamemnon\|Agamemnon]])<br>There is no tarrying here: the hart [[characters/achilles\|Achilles]]<br>Keeps thicket. Please it our great general<br>To call together all his state of war.<br>Fresh kings are come today to Troy; tomorrow<br>We must with all our main of power stand fast.<br>And here’s a lord, come knights from east to west<br>And cull their flower, [[characters/ajax\|Ajax]] shall cope the best. |
| [[characters/agamemnon\|AGAMEMNON]] | Go we to counsel. Let [[characters/achilles\|Achilles]] [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]].<br>Light boats sail swift, though greater hulks draw<br>deep. Exeunt |

### Atto 3, Scena 1

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Music sounds within. Enter [[characters/pandarus\|Pandarus]] at one door and a Servant at another door |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Friend? You. Pray you, a word. Do not you follow the young Lord [[settings/paris\|Paris]]? |
| SERVANT | Ay, sir, when he goes before me. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | You depend upon him, I mean. |
| SERVANT | Sir, I do depend upon the Lord. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | You depend upon a notable gentleman; I must needs praise him. |
| SERVANT | The Lord be praised! |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | You know me—do you not? |
| SERVANT | [[concepts/faith\|Faith]], sir, superficially. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Friend, know me better. I am the Lord [[characters/pandarus\|Pandarus]]. |
| SERVANT | I [[concepts/hope\|hope]] I shall know your [[concepts/honour\|honour]] better. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | I do [[motifs/desire\|desire]] it. |
| SERVANT | You are in the state of grace? |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Grace? Not so, friend. ‘[[concepts/honour\|Honour]]’ and ‘lordship’ are my titles. What music is this? |
| SERVANT | I do but partly know, sir. It is music in parts. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Know you the musicians? |
| SERVANT | Wholly, sir. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Who play they to? |
| SERVANT | To the hearers, sir. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | At whose pleasure, friend? |
| SERVANT | At mine, sir, and theirs that [[motifs/love\|love]] music. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | ‘Command’ I mean, friend. |
| SERVANT | Who shall I command, sir? |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Friend, we understand not one another. I am too courtly and thou too cunning. At whose request do these men play? |
| SERVANT | That’s to’t indeed, sir. Marry, sir, at the request of [[settings/paris\|Paris]] my lord, who’s there in person; with him, the mortal [[characters/venus\|Venus]], the heart-[[motifs/blood\|blood]] of [[concepts/beauty\|beauty]], [[motifs/love\|love]]’s visible [[motifs/soul\|soul]]— |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Who, my cousin [[characters/cressida\|Cressida]]? |
| SERVANT | No, sir, [[characters/helen\|Helen]]. Could not you find out that by her attributes? |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | It should seem, fellow, that thou hast not seen the Lady Cressid. I come to speak with [[settings/paris\|Paris]] from the Prince [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]]. I will make a complimental assault upon him, for my business seethes. |
| SERVANT | Sodden business! There’s a stewed phrase, indeed. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[settings/paris\|Paris]] and [[characters/helen\|Helen]], attended by musicians |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Fair be to you, my lord, and to all this fair company. Fair desires in all fair measure fairly guide them—especially to you, fair [[characters/queen\|Queen]]. Fair thoughts be your fair pillow. |
| [[characters/helen\|HELEN]] | Dear lord, you are full of fair words. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | You speak your fair pleasure, sweet [[characters/queen\|Queen]]. (To [[settings/paris\|Paris]]) Fair prince, here is good broken music. |
| [[settings/paris\|PARIS]] | You have broke it, cousin, and by my life you shall make it whole again. You shall piece it out with a piece of your performance.—Nell, he is full of harmony. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Truly, lady, no. |
| [[characters/helen\|HELEN]] | O sir. |
| *(didascalia)* | She tickles him |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Rude, in sooth, in good sooth very rude. |
| [[settings/paris\|PARIS]] | [[motifs/well\|Well]] said, my lord. Will you say so in fits? |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | I have business to my lord, dear [[characters/queen\|Queen]].—My lord, will you vouchsafe me a word? |
| [[characters/helen\|HELEN]] | Nay, this shall not hedge us out. We’ll hear you sing, certainly. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | [[motifs/well\|Well]], sweet [[characters/queen\|Queen]], you are pleasant with me.—But marry, thus, my lord: my dear lord and most esteemed friend, your brother [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]]— |
| [[characters/helen\|HELEN]] | My lord [[characters/pandarus\|Pandarus]], honey-sweet lord. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Go to, sweet [[characters/queen\|Queen]], go tot—commends himself most affectionately to you. |
| [[characters/helen\|HELEN]] | You shall not bob us out of our melody. If you do, our [[concepts/melancholy\|melancholy]] upon your head. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Sweet [[characters/queen\|Queen]], sweet [[characters/queen\|Queen]], that’s a sweet<br>[[characters/queen\|Queen]]. Ay, [[concepts/faith\|faith]]— |
| [[characters/helen\|HELEN]] | And to make a sweet lady sad is a sour offence. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Nay, that shall not serve your turn; that shall it not, in [[concepts/truth\|truth]], la. Nay, I care not for such words. No, no.—And, my lord, he desires you that, if the [[archetypes/king\|King]] call for him at supper, you will make his excuse. |
| [[characters/helen\|HELEN]] | My lord [[characters/pandarus\|Pandarus]]. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | What says my sweet [[characters/queen\|Queen]], my very very sweet [[characters/queen\|Queen]]? |
| [[settings/paris\|PARIS]] | What exploit’s in hand? Where sups he tonight? |
| [[characters/helen\|HELEN]] | Nay, but my lord— |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | What says my sweet [[characters/queen\|Queen]]? My cousin will fall out with you. |
| [[characters/helen\|HELEN]] | (to [[settings/paris\|Paris]]) You must not know where he sups. |
| [[settings/paris\|PARIS]] | I’ll lay my life, with my dispenser [[characters/cressida\|Cressida]]. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | No, no! No such matter. You are wide. Come, your dispenser is sick. |
| [[settings/paris\|PARIS]] | [[motifs/well\|Well]], I’ll make’s excuse. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Ay, good my lord. Why should you say<br>[[characters/cressida\|Cressida]]? No, your poor dispenser’s sick. |
| [[settings/paris\|PARIS]] | ‘I spy.’ |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] You spy? What do you spy?—To a musician |
| [[settings/paris\|PARIS]] | Come, give me an instrument.—Now, sweet [[characters/queen\|Queen]]. |
| [[characters/helen\|HELEN]] | Why, this is kindly done! |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | My niece is horrible in [[motifs/love\|love]] with a thing you have, sweet [[characters/queen\|Queen]]. |
| [[characters/helen\|HELEN]] | She shall have it, my lord—if it be not my lord [[settings/paris\|Paris]]. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | He? No, she’ll none of him. They two are twain. |
| [[characters/helen\|HELEN]] | Falling in, after falling out, may make them three. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Come, come, I’ll hear no more of this. I’ll sing you a song now. |
| [[characters/helen\|HELEN]] | Ay, ay, prithee. Now by my troth, sweet lord, thou hast a fine forehead. |
| *(didascalia)* | She strokes his forehead |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Ay, you may, you may. |
| [[characters/helen\|HELEN]] | Let thy song be [[motifs/love\|love]]. ‘This [[motifs/love\|love]] will undo us all.’<br>O Cupid, Cupid, Cupid! |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | [[motifs/love\|Love]]? Ay, that it shall, i’[[concepts/faith\|faith]]. |
| [[settings/paris\|PARIS]] | Ay, good now, ‘[[motifs/love\|Love]], [[motifs/love\|love]], nothing but [[motifs/love\|love]]’. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | In good [[concepts/truth\|truth]], it begins so.<br>(Sings)<br>[[motifs/love\|Love]], [[motifs/love\|love]], nothing but [[motifs/love\|love]], still [[motifs/love\|love]], still more!<br>For O [[motifs/love\|love]]’s bow<br>Shoots buck and doe.<br>The shaft confounds<br>Not that it wounds,<br>But tickles still the sore.<br>These lovers cry ‘O! O!’, they die.<br>Yet that which seems the wound to kill<br>Doth turn ‘O! O!’ to ‘ha ha he!’<br>So dying [[motifs/love\|love]] lives still.<br>‘O! O!’ a while, but ‘ha ha ha!’<br>‘O! O!’ groans out for ‘ha ha ha!’—<br>Heigh-ho. |
| [[characters/helen\|HELEN]] | In [[motifs/love\|love]]—ay, [[concepts/faith\|faith]], to the very tip of the nose. |
| [[settings/paris\|PARIS]] | He eats nothing but doves, [[motifs/love\|love]], and that breeds hot [[motifs/blood\|blood]], and hot [[motifs/blood\|blood]] begets hot thoughts, and hot thoughts beget hot deeds, and hot deeds is [[motifs/love\|love]]. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Is this the generation of [[motifs/love\|love]]: hot [[motifs/blood\|blood]], hot thoughts, and hot deeds? Why, they are vipers. Is [[motifs/love\|love]] a generation of vipers? |
| *(didascalia)* | Alarum |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Sweet lord, who’s afield today? |
| [[settings/paris\|PARIS]] | [[characters/hector\|Hector]], Deiphobus, Helenus, Antenor, and all the gallantry of Troy. I would fain have armed today, but my Nell would not have it so. How chance my brother [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]] went not? |
| [[characters/helen\|HELEN]] | He hangs the lip at something. You know all, Lord<br>[[characters/pandarus\|Pandarus]]. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Not I, honey-sweet [[characters/queen\|Queen]]. I long to hear how they sped today.—You’ll remember your brother’s excuse? |
| [[settings/paris\|PARIS]] | To a hair. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Farewell, sweet [[characters/queen\|Queen]]. |
| [[characters/helen\|HELEN]] | Commend me to your niece. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | I will, sweet [[characters/queen\|Queen]]. Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | Sound a retreat |
| [[settings/paris\|PARIS]] | They’re come from field. Let us to Priam’s hall<br>To greet the warriors. Sweet [[characters/helen\|Helen]], I must woo you<br>To help unarm our [[characters/hector\|Hector]]. His stubborn buckles,<br>With these your white enchanting fingers touched,<br>Shall more obey than to the edge of steel<br>Or force of Greekish sinews. You shall do more<br>Than all the island kings: disarm great [[characters/hector\|Hector]]. |
| [[characters/helen\|HELEN]] | ’Twill make us proud to be his servant, [[settings/paris\|Paris]];<br>Yea, what he shall receive of us in duty<br>Gives us more palm in [[concepts/beauty\|beauty]] than we have—<br>Yea, overshines ourself. |
| [[settings/paris\|PARIS]] | Sweet above thought, I [[motifs/love\|love]] thee! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 3, Scena 2

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/pandarus\|Pandarus]] at one door and [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]]’ man at another door |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | How now, where’s thy master? At my cousin [[characters/cressida\|Cressida]]’s? |
| MAN | No, sir, he stays for you to conduct him thither. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]] |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | O here he comes.—How now, how now? |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | Sirrah, walk off. Exit Man |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Have you seen my cousin? |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | No, [[characters/pandarus\|Pandarus]], I stalk about her door<br>Like a strange [[motifs/soul\|soul]] upon the Stygian banks<br>Staying for waftage. O be thou my Charon,<br>And give me swift transportance to those fields<br>Where I may wallow in the lily beds<br>Proposed for the deserver. O gentle Pandar,<br>From Cupid’s shoulder pluck his painted wings<br>And fly with me to Cressid. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Walk here i’th’ orchard. I’ll bring her straight. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | I am giddy. Expectation whirls me round.<br>Th‘imaginary relish is so sweet<br>That it enchants my sense. What will it be<br>When that the wat’ry palates taste indeed<br>[[motifs/love\|Love]]’s thrice-repurèd nectar? [[motifs/death\|Death]], I [[concepts/fear\|fear]] me,<br>Swooning destruction, or some joy too fine,<br>Too subtle-potent, tuned too sharp in sweetness<br>For the capacity of my ruder powers.<br>I [[concepts/fear\|fear]] it much, and I do [[concepts/fear\|fear]] besides<br>That I shall lose distinction in my joys,<br>As doth a battle when they charge on heaps<br>The enemy flying. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/pandarus\|Pandarus]] |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | She’s making her ready. She’ll come straight. You must be witty now. She does so blush, and fetches her [[motifs/wind\|wind]] so short as if she were frayed with a spirit. I’ll fetch her. It is the prettiest [[archetypes/villain\|villain]]! She fetches her breath as short as a new-ta’en sparrow. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | Even such a passion doth embrace my bosom.<br>My heart beats thicker than a feverous pulse,<br>And all my powers do their bestowing lose,<br>Like vassalage at unawares encount’ring<br>The eye of majesty. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/pandarus\|Pandarus]], with [[characters/cressida\|Cressida]] veiled |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] (to [[characters/cressida\|Cressida]]) Come, come, what need you blush? Shame’s a baby. (To [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]]) Here she is now. Swear the oaths now to her that you have sworn to me. (To [[characters/cressida\|Cressida]]) What, are you gone again? You must be watched ere you be made tame, must you? Come your ways, come your ways. An you draw backward, we’ll put you i‘th’ thills. (To [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]]) Why do you not speak to her? (To [[characters/cressida\|Cressida]]) Come, draw this curtain, and let’s see your picture. He unveils her Alas the day! How loath you are to offend daylight! An’t were dark, you’d close sooner. So, so. (To [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]]) Rub on, and kiss the mistress. (They kiss) How now, a kiss in fee farm! Build there, carpenter, the air is sweet. Nay, you shall fight your hearts out ere I part you. The falcon as the tercel, for all the ducks i’th’ [[motifs/river\|river]]. Go to, go to. |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | You have bereft me of all words, lady. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Words pay no debts; give her deeds. But she’ll bereave you o‘th’ deeds too, if she call your activity in question. (They kiss) What, billing again? Here’s ‘in witness whereof the parties interchangeably’. Come in, come in. I’ll go get a [[motifs/fire\|fire]]. Exit |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | Will you walk in, my lord? |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | O [[characters/cressida\|Cressida]], how often have I wished me thus. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | Wished, my lord? The gods grant—O, my lordl |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | What should they grant? What makes this pretty abruption? What too-curious dreg espies my sweet lady in the fountain of our [[motifs/love\|love]]? |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | More dregs than [[motifs/water\|water]], if my fears have eyes. |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | Fears make devils of cherubims; they never see truly. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | Blind [[concepts/fear\|fear]], that seeing reason leads, finds safer footing than blind reason, stumbling without [[concepts/fear\|fear]]. To [[concepts/fear\|fear]] the worst oft cures the worse. |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | O let my lady apprehend no [[concepts/fear\|fear]]. In all Cupid’s pageant there is presented no [[archetypes/monster\|monster]]. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | Nor nothing monstrous neither? |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | Nothing but our undertakings, when we vow to weep seas, live in [[motifs/fire\|fire]], eat rocks, tame tigers, thinking it harder for our mistress to devise imposition enough than for us to undergo any difficulty imposed. This is the monstruosity in [[motifs/love\|love]], lady—that the will is infinite and the execution confined; that the [[motifs/desire\|desire]] is boundless and the act a slave to limit. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | They say all lovers swear more performance than they are able, and yet reserve an ability that they never perform: vowing more than the perfection of ten, and discharging less than the tenth part of one. They that have the voice of lions and the act of hares, are they not monsters? |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | Are there such? Such are not we. Praise us as we are tasted; allow us as we prove. Our head shall go bare till merit [[motifs/crown\|crown]] it. No perfection in reversion shall have a praise in present. We will not name [[motifs/desert\|desert]] before his birth, and being born his addition shall be humble. Few words to fair [[concepts/faith\|faith]]. [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]] shall be such to Cressid as what envy can say worst shall be a mock for his [[concepts/truth\|truth]]; and what [[concepts/truth\|truth]] can speak truest, not truer than [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]]. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | Will you walk in, my lord? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/pandarus\|Pandarus]] |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | What, blushing still? Have you not done talking yet? |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | [[motifs/well\|Well]], uncle, what folly I commit I dedicate to you. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | I thank you for that. If my lord get a boy of you, you’ll give him me. Be true to my lord. If he flinch, chide me for it. |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | (to [[characters/cressida\|Cressida]]) You know now your hostages: your uncle’s word and my firm [[concepts/faith\|faith]]. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Nay, I’ll give my word for her too. Our kindred, though they be long ere they are wooed, they are constant being won. They are burrs, I can tell you: they’ll stick where they are thrown. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | Boldness comes to me now, and brings me heart.<br>Prince [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]], I have loved you [[motifs/night\|night]] and day<br>For many weary months. |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | Why was my Cressid then so hard to win? |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | Hard to seem won; but I was won, my lord,<br>With the first glance that ever—pardon me:<br>If I confess much, you will play the [[archetypes/tyrant\|tyrant]].<br>I [[motifs/love\|love]] you now, but till now not so much<br>But I might master it. In [[concepts/faith\|faith]], I lie:<br>My thoughts were like unbridled children, grown<br>Too headstrong for their mother. See, we fools!<br>Why have I blabbed? Who shall be true to us,<br>When we are so unsecret to ourselves?<br>But though I loved you [[motifs/well\|well]], I wooed you not—<br>And yet, good [[concepts/faith\|faith]], I wished myself a man,<br>Or that we women had men’s privilege<br>Of speaking first. Sweet, bid me hold my tongue,<br>For in this rapture I shall surely speak<br>The thing I shall repent. See, see, your [[motifs/silence\|silence]],<br>Cunning in dumbness, in my weakness draws<br>My [[motifs/soul\|soul]] of counsel from me. Stop my mouth. |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | And shall, albeit sweet music issues thence.<br>He kisses her |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Pretty, i’ [[concepts/faith\|faith]]. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | (to [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]])<br>My lord, I do beseech you pardon me.<br>’Twas not my purpose thus to beg a kiss.<br>I am ashamed. O heavens, what have I done?<br>For this [[motifs/time\|time]] will I take my leave, my lord. [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] Your leave, sweet Cressid? |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Leave? An you take leave till tomorrow morning— |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | Pray you, content you. |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | What offends you, lady? |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | Sir, mine own company. |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | You cannot shun yourself. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | Let me go and try.<br>I have a kind of [[concepts/self\|self]] resides with you—<br>But an unkind [[concepts/self\|self]], that itself will leave<br>To be another’s [[archetypes/fool\|fool]]. Where is my wit?<br>I would be gone. I speak I know not what. |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | [[motifs/well\|Well]] know they what they speak that speak so wisely. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | Perchance, my lord, I show more craft than [[motifs/love\|love]],<br>And fell so roundly to a large confession<br>To angle for your thoughts. But you are wise,<br>Or else you [[motifs/love\|love]] not—for to be wise and [[motifs/love\|love]]<br>Exceeds man’s might: that dwells with gods above. |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | O that I thought it could be in a woman—<br>As, if it can, I will presume in you—<br>To feed for aye her lamp and flames of [[motifs/love\|love]],<br>To keep her constancy in plight and youth,<br>Outliving [[concepts/beauty\|beauty]]’s outward, with a mind<br>That doth renew swifter than [[motifs/blood\|blood]] decays;<br>Or that persuasion could but thus convince me<br>That my integrity and [[concepts/truth\|truth]] to you<br>Might be affronted with the match and weight<br>Of such a winnowed purity in [[motifs/love\|love]].<br>How were I then uplifted! But alas,<br>I am as true as [[concepts/truth\|truth]]’s simplicity,<br>And simpler than the infancy of [[concepts/truth\|truth]]. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | In that I’ll war with you. |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | O virtuous fight,<br>When right with right wars who shall be most right.<br>True swains in [[motifs/love\|love]] shall in the world to come<br>Approve their [[concepts/truth\|truth]] by [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]]. When their rhymes,<br>Full of protest, of oath and big compare,<br>Wants similes, [[concepts/truth\|truth]] tired with iteration—<br>‘As true as steel, as plantage to the [[motifs/moon\|moon]],<br>As sun to day, as turtle to her mate,<br>As iron to adamant, as earth to th’ centre’—<br>Yet, after all comparisons of [[concepts/truth\|truth]],<br>As [[concepts/truth\|truth]]’s authentic author to be cited,<br>’As true as [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]]’ shall [[motifs/crown\|crown]] up the verse<br>And sanctify the numbers. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | [[archetypes/prophet\|Prophet]] may you be!<br>If I be false, or swerve a hair from [[concepts/truth\|truth]],<br>When [[motifs/time\|time]] is old and hath forgot itself,<br>When [[motifs/water\|water]] drops have worn the stones of Troy<br>And blind oblivion swallowed cities up,<br>And mighty states characterless are grated<br>To dusty nothing, yet let [[motifs/memory\|memory]]<br>From false to false among false maids in [[motifs/love\|love]]<br>Upbraid my falsehood. When they’ve said, ‘as false<br>As air, as [[motifs/water\|water]], [[motifs/wind\|wind]] or sandy earth,<br>As fox to [[motifs/lamb\|lamb]], or wolf to heifer’s calf,<br>Pard to the hind, or stepdame to her son’,<br>Yea, let them say, to stick the heart of falsehood,<br>‘As false as Cressid’. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Go to, a bargain made. Seal it, seal it. I’ll be the witness. Here I hold your hand; here, my cousin’s. If ever you prove false one to another, since I have taken such [[motifs/pain\|pain]] to bring you together, let all pitiful goers-between be called to the world’s end after my name: call them all panders. Let all constant men be Troiluses, all false women Cressids, and all brokers-between panders. Say ‘Amen’. |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | Amen. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | Amen. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Amen. Whereupon I will show you a chamber with a bed—which bed, because it shall not speak of your pretty encounters, press it to [[motifs/death\|death]]. Away! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]] and [[characters/cressida\|Cressida]] |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | And Cupid grant all tongue-tied maidens here<br>Bed, chamber, pander to provide this gear. Exit |

### Atto 3, Scena 3

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish. Enter [[characters/ulysses\|Ulysses]], Diomedes, Nestor, [[characters/agamemnon\|Agamemnon]], Menelaus, [[characters/ajax\|Ajax]], and Calchas |
| CALCHAS | Now, princes, for the service I have done you,<br>Th‘advantage of the [[motifs/time\|time]] prompts me aloud<br>To call for recompense. Appear it to your mind<br>That through the sight I bear in things to come<br>I have abandoned Troy, left my profession,<br>Incurred a traitor’s name, exposed myself<br>From certain and possessed conveniences<br>To doubtful fortunes, sequest’ring from me all<br>That [[motifs/time\|time]], acquaintance, custom, and condition<br>Made tame and most familiar to my [[concepts/nature\|nature]],<br>And here to do you service am become<br>As new into the world, strange, unacquainted.<br>I do beseech you, as in way of taste,<br>To give me now a little benefit<br>Out of those many registered in promise<br>Which you say live to come in my behalf. |
| [[characters/agamemnon\|AGAMEMNON]] | What wouldst thou of us, Trojan? Make demand. |
| CALCHAS | You have a Trojan prisoner called Antenor,<br>Yesterday took. Troy holds him very dear.<br>Oft have you—often have you thanks therefor—<br>Desired my Cressid in right great exchange,<br>Whom Troy hath still denied. But this Antenor<br>I know is such a wrest in their affairs<br>That their negotiations all must slack,<br>Wanting his manage, and they will almost<br>Give us a prince of [[motifs/blood\|blood]], a son of Priam,<br>In change of him. Let him be sent, great princes,<br>And he shall buy my daughter, and her presence<br>Shall quite strike off all service I have done<br>In most accepted [[motifs/pain\|pain]]. |
| [[characters/agamemnon\|AGAMEMNON]] | Let Diomedes bear him,<br>And bring us Cressid hither; Calchas shall have<br>What he requests of us. Good Diomed,<br>Furnish you fairly for this interchange;<br>Withal bring word if [[characters/hector\|Hector]] will tomorrow<br>Be answered in his challenge. [[characters/ajax\|Ajax]] is ready. |
| DIOMEDES | This shall I undertake, and ’tis a burden<br>Which I am proud to bear. Exit with Calchas |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/achilles\|Achilles]] and Patroclus in their tent |
| [[characters/ulysses\|ULYSSES]] | [[characters/achilles\|Achilles]] stands i‘th’ entrance of his tent.<br>Please it our general pass strangely by him,<br>As if he were forgot; and, princes all,<br>Lay negligent and loose regard upon him.<br>I will come last. ’Tis like he’ll question me<br>Why such unplausive eyes are bent, why turned on<br>him.<br>If so, I have derision medicinable<br>To use between your strangeness and his [[concepts/pride\|pride]],<br>Which his own will shall have [[motifs/desire\|desire]] to drink.<br>It may do good. [[concepts/pride\|Pride]] hath no other glass<br>To show itself but [[concepts/pride\|pride]]; for supple knees<br>Feed arrogance and are the proud man’s fees. |
| [[characters/agamemnon\|AGAMEMNON]] | We’ll execute your purpose and put on<br>A form of strangeness as we pass along.<br>So do each lord, and either greet him not<br>Or else disdainfully, which shall shake him more<br>Than if not looked on. I will lead the way.<br>They pass by the tent, in turn |
| [[characters/achilles\|ACHILLES]] | What, comes the general to speak with me?<br>You know my mind: I’ll fight no more ’gainst Troy. |
| [[characters/agamemnon\|AGAMEMNON]] | (to Nestor)<br>What says [[characters/achilles\|Achilles]]? Would he aught with us? |
| NESTOR | (to [[characters/achilles\|Achilles]])<br>Would you, my lord, aught with the general? |
| [[characters/achilles\|ACHILLES]] | No. |
| NESTOR | (to [[characters/agamemnon\|Agamemnon]])<br>Nothing, my lord. |
| [[characters/agamemnon\|AGAMEMNON]] | The better. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[characters/agamemnon\|Agamemnon]] and Nestor |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/achilles\|ACHILLES]] to Menelaus Good day, good day. |
| MENELAUS | How do you? How do you? |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| [[characters/achilles\|ACHILLES]] | (to Patroclus)<br>What, does the cuckold scorn me? |
| [[characters/ajax\|AJAX]] | How now, Patroclus? |
| [[characters/achilles\|ACHILLES]] | Good morrow, [[characters/ajax\|Ajax]]. |
| [[characters/ajax\|AJAX]] | Ha? |
| [[characters/achilles\|ACHILLES]] | Good morrow. |
| [[characters/ajax\|AJAX]] | Ay, and good next day too. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| [[characters/achilles\|ACHILLES]] | (to Patroclus)<br>What mean these fellows? Know they not [[characters/achilles\|Achilles]]? |
| PATROCLUS | They pass by strangely. They were used to bend,<br>To send their smiles before them to [[characters/achilles\|Achilles]],<br>To come as humbly as they use to creep<br>To holy altars. |
| [[characters/achilles\|ACHILLES]] | What, am I poor of late?<br>‘Tis certain, [[concepts/greatness\|greatness]] once fall’n out with fortune<br>Must fall out with men too. What the declined is<br>He shall as soon read in the eyes of others<br>As feel in his own fall; for men, like butterflies,<br>Show not their mealy wings but to the [[motifs/summer\|summer]],<br>And not a man, for being simply man,<br>Hath any [[concepts/honour\|honour]], but [[concepts/honour\|honour]] for those honours<br>That are without him—as place, riches, and favour:<br>Prizes of accident as oft as merit;<br>Which, when they fall, as being slippery standers—<br>The [[motifs/love\|love]] that leaned on them, as slippery too—<br>Doth one pluck down another, and together<br>Die in the fall. But ’tis not so with me.<br>Fortune and I are friends. I do enjoy<br>At ample point all that I did possess,<br>Save these men’s looks—who do methinks find out<br>Something not worth in me such rich beholding<br>As they have often given. Here is [[characters/ulysses\|Ulysses]];<br>I’ll interrupt his reading. How now, [[characters/ulysses\|Ulysses]]? |
| [[characters/ulysses\|ULYSSES]] | Now, great Thetis’ son. |
| [[characters/achilles\|ACHILLES]] | What are you reading? |
| [[characters/ulysses\|ULYSSES]] | A strange fellow here<br>Writes me that man, how dearly ever parted,<br>How much in having, or without or in,<br>Cannot make boast to have that which he hath,<br>Nor feels not what he owes, but by reflection—<br>As when his virtues, shining upon others,<br>Heat them, and they retort that heat again<br>To the first givers. |
| [[characters/achilles\|ACHILLES]] | This is not strange, [[characters/ulysses\|Ulysses]].<br>The [[concepts/beauty\|beauty]] that is borne here in the face<br>The bearer knows not, but commends itself<br>To others’ eyes. Nor doth the eye itself,<br>That most pure spirit of sense, behold itself,<br>Not going from itself; but eye to eye opposed<br>Salutes each other with each other’s form.<br>For speculation turns not to itself<br>Till it hath travelled and is mirrored there<br>Where it may see itself. This is not strange at all. |
| [[characters/ulysses\|ULYSSES]] | I do not strain at the position—<br>It is familiar—but at the author’s drift;<br>Who in his circumstance expressly proves<br>That no man is the lord of anything,<br>Though in and of him there be much consisting,<br>Till he communicate his parts to others.<br>Nor doth he of himself know them for aught<br>Till he behold them formed in th‘applause<br>Where they’re extended—who, like an arch, reverb’rate<br>The voice again; or, like a gate of steel<br>Fronting the sun, receives and renders back<br>His figure and his heat. I was much rapt in this,<br>And apprehended here immediately<br>The unknown [[characters/ajax\|Ajax]].<br>Heavens, what a man is there! A very [[motifs/horse\|horse]],<br>That has he knows not what. [[concepts/nature\|Nature]], what things<br>there are,<br>Most abject in regard and dear in use.<br>What things again, most dear in the esteem<br>And poor in worth. Now shall we see tomorrow<br>An act that very chance doth throw upon him.<br>[[characters/ajax\|Ajax]] renowned? O heavens, what some men do,<br>While some men leave to do.<br>How some men creep in skittish Fortune’s hall<br>Whiles others play the idiots in her eyes;<br>How one man eats into another’s [[concepts/pride\|pride]]<br>While [[concepts/pride\|pride]] is fasting in his wantonness.<br>To see these Grecian lords! Why, even already<br>They clap the lubber [[characters/ajax\|Ajax]] on the shoulder,<br>As if his foot were on brave [[characters/hector\|Hector]]’s breast<br>And great Troy shrinking. |
| [[characters/achilles\|ACHILLES]] | I do believe it,<br>For they passed by me as misers do by beggars,<br>Neither gave to me good word nor look.<br>What, are my deeds forgot? |
| [[characters/ulysses\|ULYSSES]] | [[motifs/time\|Time]] hath, my lord,<br>A wallet at his back, wherein he puts<br>Alms for oblivion, a great-sized [[archetypes/monster\|monster]]<br>Of ingratitudes. Those scraps are good deeds past,<br>Which are devoured as fast as they are made,<br>Forgot as soon as done. Perseverance, dear my lord,<br>Keeps [[concepts/honour\|honour]] bright. To have done is to hang<br>Quite out of fashion, like a rusty mail<br>In monumental mock‘ry. Take the instant way,<br>For [[concepts/honour\|honour]] travels in a strait so narrow,<br>Where one but goes abreast. Keep then the path,<br>For emulation hath a thousand sons<br>That one by one pursue: if you give way,<br>Or hedge aside from the direct forthright,<br>Like to an entered tide they all rush by<br>And leave you hindmost;<br>Or, like a gallant [[motifs/horse\|horse]] fall’n in first rank,<br>Lie there for pavement to the abject rear,<br>O’errun and trampled on. Then what they do in<br>present,<br>Though less than yours in past, must o‘ertop yours.<br>For [[motifs/time\|Time]] is like a fashionable host,<br>That slightly shakes his parting guest by th’ hand<br>And, with his arms outstretched as he would fly,<br>Grasps in the comer. Welcome ever smiles,<br>And Farewell goes out sighing. O let not virtue seek<br>Remuneration for the thing it was;<br>For [[concepts/beauty\|beauty]], wit,<br>High birth, vigour of bone, [[motifs/desert\|desert]] in service,<br>[[motifs/love\|Love]], [[concepts/friendship\|friendship]], [[concepts/charity\|charity]], are subjects all<br>To envious and calumniating [[motifs/time\|time]].<br>One touch of [[concepts/nature\|nature]] makes the whole world kin—<br>That all with one consent praise new-born gauds,<br>Though they are made and moulded of things past,<br>And give to [[motifs/dust\|dust]] that is a little gilt<br>More laud than gilt o’er-dusted.<br>The present eye praises the present object.<br>Then marvel not, thou great and complete man,<br>That all the Greeks begin to worship [[characters/ajax\|Ajax]],<br>Since things in motion sooner catch the eye<br>Than what not stirs. The cry went once on thee,<br>And still it might, and yet it may again,<br>If thou wouldst not entomb thyself alive<br>And case thy reputation in thy tent,<br>Whose glorious deeds but in these fields of late<br>Made emulous missions ’mongst the gods themselves,<br>And drove great Mars to faction. |
| [[characters/achilles\|ACHILLES]] | Of this my privacy<br>I have strong reasons. |
| [[characters/ulysses\|ULYSSES]] | But ’gainst your privacy<br>The reasons are more potent and heroical.<br>’Tis known, [[characters/achilles\|Achilles]], that you are in [[motifs/love\|love]]<br>With one of Priam’s daughters. |
| [[characters/achilles\|ACHILLES]] | Ha? Known? |
| [[characters/ulysses\|ULYSSES]] | Is that a [[concepts/wonder\|wonder]]?<br>The [[concepts/providence\|providence]] that’s in a watchful state<br>Knows almost every grain of Pluto’s gold,<br>Finds [[characters/bottom\|bottom]] in th’uncomprehensive deeps,<br>Keeps place with aught, and almost like the gods<br>Do infant thoughts unveil in their dumb cradles.<br>There is a mystery, with whom relation<br>Durst never meddle, in the [[motifs/soul\|soul]] of state,<br>Which hath an operation more divine<br>Than breath or pen can give expressure to.<br>All the commerce that you have had with Troy<br>As perfectly is ours as yours, my lord;<br>And better would it fit [[characters/achilles\|Achilles]] much<br>To throw down [[characters/hector\|Hector]] than Polyxena.<br>But it must grieve young Pyrrhus now at [[motifs/home\|home]],<br>When fame shall in his island sound her trump<br>And all the Greekish girls shall tripping sing,<br>’Great [[characters/hector\|Hector]]’s sister did [[characters/achilles\|Achilles]] win,<br>But our great [[characters/ajax\|Ajax]] bravely beat down him’.<br>Farewell, my lord. I as your [[archetypes/lover\|lover]] speak.<br>The [[archetypes/fool\|fool]] slides o’er the ice that you should break. Exit |
| PATROCLUS | To this effect, [[characters/achilles\|Achilles]], have I moved you.<br>A woman impudent and mannish grown<br>Is not more loathed than an effeminate man<br>In [[motifs/time\|time]] of action. I stand condemned for this.<br>They think my little stomach to the war<br>And your great [[motifs/love\|love]] to me restrains you thus.<br>Sweet, rouse yourself, and the weak wanton Cupid<br>Shall from your neck unloose his amorous fold<br>And like a dew-drop from the [[motifs/lion\|lion]]’s mane<br>Be shook to air. |
| [[characters/achilles\|ACHILLES]] | Shall [[characters/ajax\|Ajax]] fight with [[characters/hector\|Hector]]? |
| PATROCLUS | Ay, and perhaps receive much [[concepts/honour\|honour]] by him. |
| [[characters/achilles\|ACHILLES]] | I see my reputation is at stake.<br>My fame is shrewdly gored. |
| PATROCLUS | O then beware:<br>Those wounds heal ill that men do give themselves.<br>Omission to do what is necessary<br>Seals a commission to a blank of danger,<br>And danger like an ague subtly taints<br>Even then when we sit idly in the sun. |
| [[characters/achilles\|ACHILLES]] | Go call [[characters/thersites\|Thersites]] hither, sweet Patroclus.<br>I’ll send the [[archetypes/fool\|fool]] to [[characters/ajax\|Ajax]], and [[motifs/desire\|desire]] him<br>T’invite the Trojan lords after the combat<br>To see us here unarmed. I have a woman’s [[concepts/longing\|longing]],<br>An appetite that I am sick withal,<br>To see great [[characters/hector\|Hector]] in his weeds of peace, |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/thersites\|Thersites]] |
| [[characters/achilles\|ACHILLES]] | To talk with him and to behold his visage<br>Even to my full of view.—A labour saved. |
| [[characters/thersites\|THERSITES]] | A [[concepts/wonder\|wonder]]! |
| [[characters/achilles\|ACHILLES]] | What? |
| [[characters/thersites\|THERSITES]] | [[characters/ajax\|Ajax]] goes up and down the field, as asking for himself. |
| [[characters/achilles\|ACHILLES]] | How so? |
| [[characters/thersites\|THERSITES]] | He must fight singly tomorrow with [[characters/hector\|Hector]], and is so prophetically proud of an heroical cudgelling that he raves in saying nothing. |
| [[characters/achilles\|ACHILLES]] | How can that be? |
| [[characters/thersites\|THERSITES]] | Why, a stalks up and down like a peacock—a stride and a stand; ruminates like an hostess that hath no arithmetic but her brain to set down her reckoning; bites his lip with a politic regard, as who should say ‘There were wit in this head, an’t would out’—and so there is; but it lies as coldly in him as [[motifs/fire\|fire]] in a flint, which will not show without knocking. The man’s undone for ever, for if [[characters/hector\|Hector]] break not his neck i‘th’ combat he’ll break’t himself in vainglory. He knows not me. I said, ‘Good morrow, [[characters/ajax\|Ajax]]’, and he replies, ‘Thanks, [[characters/agamemnon\|Agamemnon]]’. What think you of this man that takes me for the General? He’s grown a very land-fish, languageless, a [[archetypes/monster\|monster]]. A plague of opinion! A man may wear it on both sides like a leather jerkin. |
| [[characters/achilles\|ACHILLES]] | Thou must be my ambassador to him, [[characters/thersites\|Thersites]]. |
| [[characters/thersites\|THERSITES]] | Who, I? Why, he’ll answer nobody. He professes not answering. Speaking is for beggars. He wears his tongue in’s arms. I will put on his presence. Let Patroclus make demands to me. You shall see the pageant of [[characters/ajax\|Ajax]]. |
| [[characters/achilles\|ACHILLES]] | To him, Patroclus. Tell him I humbly [[motifs/desire\|desire]] the valiant [[characters/ajax\|Ajax]] to invite the most valorous [[characters/hector\|Hector]] to come unarmed to my tent, and to procure safe-conduct for his person of the magnanimous and most illustrious six-or-seven-times-honoured captain-general of the Grecian army, [[characters/agamemnon\|Agamemnon]]; et cetera. Do this. |
| PATROCLUS | (to [[characters/thersites\|Thersites]]) Jove bless great [[characters/ajax\|Ajax]]! |
| [[characters/thersites\|THERSITES]] | H’m. |
| PATROCLUS | I come from the worthy [[characters/achilles\|Achilles]]— |
| [[characters/thersites\|THERSITES]] | Ha? |
| PATROCLUS | Who most humbly desires you to invite [[characters/hector\|Hector]] to his tent— |
| [[characters/thersites\|THERSITES]] | H’m! |
| PATROCLUS | And to procure safe-conduct from [[characters/agamemnon\|Agamemnon]]. |
| [[characters/thersites\|THERSITES]] | [[characters/agamemnon\|Agamemnon]]? |
| PATROCLUS | Ay, my lord. |
| [[characters/thersites\|THERSITES]] | Ha! |
| PATROCLUS | What say you to’t? |
| [[characters/thersites\|THERSITES]] | God b’wi’ you, with all my heart. |
| PATROCLUS | Your answer, sir? |
| [[characters/thersites\|THERSITES]] | If tomorrow be a fair day, by eleven o’[[motifs/clock\|clock]] it will go one way or other. Howsoever, he shall pay for me ere he has me. |
| PATROCLUS | Your answer, sir? |
| [[characters/thersites\|THERSITES]] | Fare ye [[motifs/well\|well]], with all my heart. |
| [[characters/achilles\|ACHILLES]] | Why, but he is not in this tune, is he? |
| [[characters/thersites\|THERSITES]] | No, but he’s out o’ tune thus. What music will be in him when [[characters/hector\|Hector]] has knocked out his brains, I know not. But I am feared none, unless the fiddler [[characters/apollo\|Apollo]] get his sinews to make catlings on. |
| [[characters/achilles\|ACHILLES]] | Come, thou shalt bear a letter to him straight. |
| [[characters/thersites\|THERSITES]] | Let me carry another to his [[motifs/horse\|horse]], for that’s the more capable creature. |
| [[characters/achilles\|ACHILLES]] | My mind is troubled like a fountain stirred,<br>And I myself see not the [[characters/bottom\|bottom]] of it. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit with Patroclus |
| [[characters/thersites\|THERSITES]] | Would the fountain of your mind were clear again, that I might [[motifs/water\|water]] an ass at it. I had rather be a tick in a sheep than such a valiant ignorance. Exit |

### Atto 4, Scena 1

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter at one door Aeneas with a torch; at another [[settings/paris\|Paris]], Deiphobus, Antenor, and Diomedes the Grecian, with torch-bearers |
| [[settings/paris\|PARIS]] | See, hol Who is that there? |
| DEIPHOBUS | It is the Lord Aeneas. |
| AENEAS | Is the Prince there in person? Had I so good occasion to lie long<br>As you, Prince [[settings/paris\|Paris]], nothing but heavenly business<br>Should rob my bed-mate of my company. |
| DIOMEDES | That’s my mind too. Good morrow, Lord Aeneas. |
| [[settings/paris\|PARIS]] | A valiant Greek, Aeneas, take his hand.<br>Witness the process of your speech, wherein<br>You told how Diomed e’en a whole week by days<br>Did haunt you in the field. |
| AENEAS | (to Diomedes) Health to you, valiant sir,<br>During all question of the gentle truce.<br>But when I meet you armed, as black defiance<br>As heart can think or [[concepts/courage\|courage]] execute. |
| DIOMEDES | The one and other Diomed embraces.<br>Our bloods are now in calm; and so long, health.<br>But when contention and occasion meet,<br>By Jove I’ll play the hunter for thy life<br>With all my force, pursuit, and policy. |
| AENEAS | And thou shalt hunt a [[motifs/lion\|lion]] that will fly<br>With his face backward. In humane gentleness,<br>Welcome to Troy. Now by Anchises’ life,<br>Welcome indeed) By [[characters/venus\|Venus]]’ hand I swear<br>No man alive can [[motifs/love\|love]] in such a sort<br>The thing he means to kill more excellently. |
| DIOMEDES | We sympathize. Jove, let Aeneas live—<br>If to my sword his fate be not the glory—<br>A thousand complete courses of the sun;<br>But, in mine emulous [[concepts/honour\|honour]], let him die<br>With every joint a wound—and that, tomorrow. |
| AENEAS | We know each other [[motifs/well\|well]]. |
| DIOMEDES | We do, and long to know each other worse. |
| [[settings/paris\|PARIS]] | This is the most despitefull‘st gentle greeting,<br>The noblest hateful [[motifs/love\|love]], that e’er I heard of.<br>What business, lord, so early? |
| AENEAS | I was sent for to the [[archetypes/king\|King]]; but why, I know not. |
| [[settings/paris\|PARIS]] | His purpose meets you: ’twas to bring this Greek<br>To Calchas’ house, and there to render him,<br>For the enfreed Antenor, the fair Cressid.<br>Let’s have your company, or if you please |
| *(didascalia)* | Haste there before us. Aside I constantly do think— |
| [[settings/paris\|PARIS]] | Or rather, call my thought a certain knowledge—<br>My brother [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]] lodges there tonight.<br>Rouse him and give him note of our approach,<br>With the whole quality wherefore. I [[concepts/fear\|fear]]<br>We shall be much unwelcome. |
| *(didascalia)* | AENEAS aside That I assure you. |
| [[settings/paris\|PARIS]] | [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]] had rather Troy were borne to Greece<br>Than Cressid borne from Troy. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[settings/paris\|PARIS]] aside |
| [[settings/paris\|PARIS]] | There is no help.<br>The bitter disposition of the [[motifs/time\|time]]<br>Will have it so. |
| *(didascalia)* | Aloud On, lord, we’ll follow you. |
| AENEAS | Good morrow all. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| [[settings/paris\|PARIS]] | And tell me, noble Diomed—[[concepts/faith\|faith]], tell me true,<br>Even in the [[motifs/soul\|soul]] of sound good-fellowship-<br>Who in your thoughts merits fair [[characters/helen\|Helen]] most,<br>Myself or Menelaus? |
| DIOMEDES | Both alike.<br>He merits [[motifs/well\|well]] to have her that doth seek her,<br>Not making any scruple of her soilure,<br>With such a hell of [[motifs/pain\|pain]] and world of charge;<br>And you as [[motifs/well\|well]] to keep her that defend her,<br>Not palating the taste of her dishonour,<br>With such a costly loss of wealth and friends.<br>He like a puling cuckold would drink up<br>The lees and dregs of a flat ’tamed piece;<br>You like a lecher out of whorish loins<br>Are pleased to breed out your inheritors.<br>Both merits poised, each weighs nor less nor more,<br>But he as he: which heavier for a whore? |
| [[settings/paris\|PARIS]] | You are too bitter to your countrywoman. |
| DIOMEDES | She’s bitter to her country. Hear me, [[settings/paris\|Paris]].<br>For every false drop in her bawdy veins<br>A Grecian’s life hath sunk; for every scruple<br>Of her contaminated carrion weight<br>A Trojan hath been slain. Since she could speak<br>She hath not given so many good words breath<br>As, for her, Greeks and Trojans suffered [[motifs/death\|death]]. |
| [[settings/paris\|PARIS]] | Fair Diomed, you do as chapmen do:<br>Dispraise the thing that you [[motifs/desire\|desire]] to buy.<br>But we in [[motifs/silence\|silence]] hold this virtue [[motifs/well\|well]]:<br>We’ll but commend what we intend to sell.—<br>Here lies our way. Exeunt |

### Atto 4, Scena 2

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]] and [[characters/cressida\|Cressida]] |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | Dear, trouble not yourself. The morn is cold. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | Then, sweet my lord, I’ll call mine uncle down.<br>He shall unbolt the gates. |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | Trouble him not.<br>To bed, to bed! [[motifs/sleep\|Sleep]] lull those pretty eyes<br>And give as soft attachment to thy senses<br>As to infants empty of all thought. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | Good morrow, then. |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | I prithee now, to bed. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | Are you aweary of me? |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | O [[characters/cressida\|Cressida]]! But that the busy day,<br>Waked by the lark, hath roused the ribald crows,<br>And dreaming [[motifs/night\|night]] will hide our joys no longer,<br>I would not from thee. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | [[motifs/night\|Night]] hath been too brief. |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | Beshrew the [[characters/witch\|witch]]! With venomous wights she stays<br>As hideously as hell, but flies the grasps of [[motifs/love\|love]]<br>With wings more momentary-swift than thought.<br>You will catch cold and curse me. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | Prithee, tarry. You men will never tarry.<br>O foolish Cressid! I might have still held off,<br>And then you would have tarried.—Hark, there’s one<br>up. |
| *(didascalia)* | She veils herself |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | (within) What’s all the doors open here? |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | It is your uncle. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | A pestilence on him! Now will he be mocking.<br>I shall have such a life. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/pandarus\|Pandarus]] |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | How now, how now, how go maidenheads? (To [[characters/cressida\|Cressida]]) Here, you, maid! Where’s my cousin Cressid? |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] unveiling |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Go hang yourself. You naughty, mocking uncle!<br>You bring me to do—and then you flout me too. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | To do what? To do what?—Let her say what.—What have I brought you to do? |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | Come, come, beshrew your heart. You’ll ne’er be<br>good,<br>Nor suffer others. |
| PINDARUS | Ha ha! Alas, poor wretch. Ah, poor capocchia, hast not slept tonight? Would he not—a naughty man—let it [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]]? A bugbear take him. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | (to [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]])<br>Did not I tell you? Would he were knocked i’th’ head. |
| *(didascalia)* | One knocks within |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | Who’s that at door?—Good uncle, go and see.—<br>My lord, come you again into my chamber.<br>You smile and mock me, as if I meant naughtily. |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | Ha ha! |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | Come, you are deceived, I think of no such thing.<br>One knocks within<br><br>How earnestly they knock! Pray you come in.<br>I would not for half Troy have you seen here. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]] and [[characters/cressida\|Cressida]] |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Who’s there? What’s the matter? Will you beat down the door? |
| *(didascalia)* | He opens the door. Enter Aeneas |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | How now, what’s the matter? |
| AENEAS | Good morrow, lord, good morrow. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Who’s there? My Lord Aeneas? By my troth,<br>I knew you not. What news with you so early? |
| AENEAS | Is not Prince [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]] here? |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Here? What should he do here? |
| AENEAS | Come, he is here, my lord. Do not deny him.<br>It doth import him much to speak with me. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Is he here, say you? It’s more than I know, I’ll be sworn. For my own part, I came in late. What should he do here? |
| AENEAS | Whoa! Nay, then. Come, come, you’ll do him wrong<br>Ere you are ware. You’ll be so true to him<br>To be false to him. Do not you know of him, |
| *(didascalia)* | But yet go fetch him hither. Go. Exit [[characters/pandarus\|Pandarus]] |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]] |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | How now, what’s the matter? |
| AENEAS | My lord, I scarce have leisure to salute you,<br>My matter is so rash. There is at hand<br>[[settings/paris\|Paris]] your brother and Deiphobus,<br>The Grecian Diomed, and our Antenor<br>Delivered to us—and for him forthwith,<br>Ere the first sacrifice, within this hour,<br>We must give up to Diomedes’ hand<br>The Lady [[characters/cressida\|Cressida]]. |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | Is it so concluded? |
| AENEAS | By Priam and the general state of Troy.<br>They are at hand, and ready to effect it. |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | How my achievements mock me.<br>I will go meet them—and, my Lord Aeneas,<br>We met by chance: you did not find me here. |
| AENEAS | Good, good, my lord: the secrecies of [[concepts/nature\|nature]]<br>Have not more gift in taciturnity. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 4, Scena 3

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/pandarus\|Pandarus]] and [[characters/cressida\|Cressida]] |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Is’t possible? No sooner got but lost. The devil take Antenor! The young prince will go mad. A plague upon Antenor! I would they had broke’s neck. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | How now? What’s the matter? Who was here? |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Ah, ah! |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | Why sigh you so profoundly? Where’s my lord?<br>Gone? Tell me, sweet uncle, what’s the matter? |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Would I were as deep under the earth as I am above. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | O the gods! What’s the matter? |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Pray thee, get thee in. Would thou hadst ne’er been born. I knew thou wouldst be his [[motifs/death\|death]]. O poor gentleman! A plague upon Antenor! |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | Good uncle, I beseech you on my knees; I beseech you, what’s the matter? |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Thou must be gone, wench, thou must be gone. Thou art changed for Antenor. Thou must to thy father, and be gone from [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]]. ‘Twill be his [[motifs/death\|death]]. ’Twill be his bane. He cannot bear it. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | O you immortal gods! I will not go. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Thou must. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | I will not, uncle. I have forgot my father.<br>I know no touch of consanguinity,<br>No kin, no [[motifs/love\|love]], no [[motifs/blood\|blood]], no [[motifs/soul\|soul]], so near me<br>As the sweet [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]]. O you gods divine,<br>Make Cressid’s name the very [[motifs/crown\|crown]] of falsehood<br>If ever she leave [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]]. [[motifs/time\|Time]], force, and [[motifs/death\|death]]<br>Do to this [[concepts/body\|body]] what extremity you can,<br>But the strong base and building of my [[motifs/love\|love]]<br>Is as the very centre of the earth,<br>Drawing all things to it. I’ll go in and weep— |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Do, do. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | Tear my bright hair, and scratch my praised cheeks,<br>Crack my clear voice with sobs, and break my heart<br>With sounding ‘[[characters/troilus\|Troilus]]’. I will not go from Troy. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 4, Scena 4

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[settings/paris\|Paris]], [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]], Aeneas, Deiphobus, Antenor, and Diomedes |
| [[settings/paris\|PARIS]] | It is great morning, and the hour prefixed<br>Of her delivery to this valiant Greek<br>Comes fast upon us. Good my brother [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]],<br>Tell you the lady what she is to do,<br>And haste her to the purpose. |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | Walk into her house.<br>I’ll bring her to the Grecian presently—<br>And to his hand when I deliver her,<br>Think it an altar, and thy brother [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]]<br>A priest, there off’ring to it his own heart. |
| [[settings/paris\|PARIS]] | I know what ’tis to [[motifs/love\|love]],<br>And would, as I shall pity, I could help.— |
| *(didascalia)* | Please you walk in, my lords? Exeunt |

### Atto 4, Scena 5

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/pandarus\|Pandarus]] and [[characters/cressida\|Cressida]] |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Be moderate, be moderate. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | Why tell you me of moderation?<br>The grief is fine, full, perfect that I taste,<br>And violenteth in a sense as strong<br>As that which causeth it. How can I moderate it?<br>If I could temporize with my affection<br>Or brew it to a weak and colder palate,<br>The like allayment could I give my grief.<br>My [[motifs/love\|love]] admits no qualifying dross;<br>No more my grief, in such a precious loss. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]] |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Here, here, here he comes. Ah, sweet ducks! |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | (embracing him) O [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]], [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]]! |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | What a pair of spectacles is here! Let me<br>embrace you too. ‘O heart‘, as the goodly saying is,<br>‘O heart, heavy heart,<br>Why sigh’st thou without breaking?’<br>where he answers again<br>‘Because thou canst not ease thy smart<br>By [[concepts/friendship\|friendship]] nor by speaking.’<br>There was never a truer rhyme. Let us cast away<br>nothing, for we may live to have need of such a verse.<br>We see it, we see it. How now, lambs? |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | Cressid, I [[motifs/love\|love]] thee in so strained a purity<br>That the blest gods, as angry with my fancy—<br>More bright in zeal than the devotion which<br>Cold lips blow to their deities—take thee from me. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | Have the gods envy? |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Ay, ay, ay, ay, ’tis too plain a case. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | And is it true that I must go from Troy? |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | A hateful [[concepts/truth\|truth]]. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | What, and from [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]] too? |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | From Troy and [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]]. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | Is’t possible? |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | And suddenty—where injury of chance<br>Puts back leave-taking, jostles roughly by<br>All [[motifs/time\|time]] of pause, rudely beguiles our lips<br>Of all rejoindure, forcibly prevents<br>Our locked embrasures, strangles our dear vows<br>Even in the birth of our own labouring breath.<br>We two, that with so many thousand sighs<br>Did buy each other, must poorly sell ourselves<br>With the rude brevity and discharge of one.<br>Injurious [[motifs/time\|Time]] now with a robber’s haste<br>Crams his rich thiev’ry up, he knows not how.<br>As many farewells as be [[motifs/stars\|stars]] in heaven,<br>With distinct breath and consigned kisses to them,<br>He fumbles up into a loose adieu<br>And scants us with a single famished kiss,<br>Distasted with the salt of broken [[motifs/tears\|tears]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Aeneas |
| AENEAS | My lord, is the lady ready? |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | (to [[characters/cressida\|Cressida]])<br>Hark, you are called. Some say the genius so<br>Cries ‘Come!’ to him that instantly must die. |
| *(didascalia)* | To [[characters/pandarus\|Pandarus]] Bid them have patience. She shall come |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | anon. |
| [[characters/pandarus\|PANDARUS]] | Where are my [[motifs/tears\|tears]]? Rain, to lay this [[motifs/wind\|wind]], or my heart will be blown up by the root. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit with Aeneas |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | I must then to the Grecians. |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | No remedy. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | A woeful Cressid ’mongst the merry Greeks!<br>When shall we see again? |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | Hear me, my [[motifs/love\|love]]: be thou but true of heart— |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | I true? How now! What wicked deem is this? |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | Nay, we must use expostulation kindly,<br>For it is parting from us.<br>I speak not ‘Be thou true’ as fearing thee—<br>For I will throw my glove to [[motifs/death\|Death]] himself<br>That there’s no maculation in thy heart—<br>But ‘Be thou true’ say I, to fashion in<br>My sequent protestation: ‘Be thou true,<br>And I will see thee’. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | O you shall be exposed, my lord, to dangers<br>As infinite as imminent. But I’ll be true. |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | And I’ll grow friend with danger. Wear this sleeve. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | And you this glove. When shall I see you? |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | I will corrupt the Grecian sentinels<br>To give thee nightly visitation.<br>But yet, be true. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | O heavens! ’Be true’ again! |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | Hear why I speak it, [[motifs/love\|love]].<br>The Grecian youths are full of quality,<br>Their loving [[motifs/well\|well]] composed, with gifts of [[concepts/nature\|nature]]<br>flowing,<br>And swelling o’er with arts and exercise.<br>How novelty may move, and parts with person,<br>Alas, a kind of godly [[concepts/jealousy\|jealousy]]—<br>Which I beseech you call a virtuous sin—<br>Makes me afeard. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | O heavens, you [[motifs/love\|love]] me not! |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | Die I a [[archetypes/villain\|villain]] then!<br>In this I do not call your [[concepts/faith\|faith]] in question<br>So mainly as my merit. I cannot sing,<br>Nor heel the high lavolt, nor sweeten talk,<br>Nor play at subtle games—fair virtues all,<br>To which the Grecians are most prompt and<br>pregnant.<br>But I can tell that in each grace of these<br>There lurks a still and dumb-discoursive devil<br>That tempts most cunningly. But be not tempted. |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | Do you think I will? |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | No, but something may be done that we will not,<br>And sometimes we are devils to ourselves,<br>When we will tempt the frailty of our powers,<br>Presuming on their changeful potency. |
| AENEAS | (within)<br>Nay, good my lord! |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | Come, kiss, and let us part. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[settings/paris\|PARIS]] art the door |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | Brother [[characters/troilus\|Troilus]]? |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | Good brother, come you hither,<br>And bring Aeneas and the Grecian with you. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[settings/paris\|Paris]] |
| [[characters/cressida\|CRESSIDA]] | My lord, will you be true? |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | Who, I? Alas, it is my vice, my fault.<br>Whiles others fish with craft for great opinion,<br>I with great [[concepts/truth\|truth]] catch mere simplicity;<br>Whilst some with cunning gild their copper crowns,<br>With [[concepts/truth\|truth]] and plainness I do wear mine bare. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[settings/paris\|Paris]], Aeneas, Antenor, Deiphobus, and Diomedes |
| [[characters/troilus\|TROILUS]] | [[concepts/fear\|Fear]] not my [[concepts/truth\|truth]]. The moral of my wit<br>Is ‘plain and true!’; there’s all the reach of it.—<br>Welcome, Sir Diomed. Here is the lady<br>Which for Antenor we deliver you.<br>At the port, lord, I’ll give her to thy hand,<br>And by the way possess thee what she is.<br>Entreat her fair, and by my [[motifs/soul\|soul]], fair Greek,<br>If e’er thou stand at [[concepts/mercy\|mercy]] of my sword,<br>Name Cressid, and thy life shall be as safe<br>As Priam is in Ilium. |
| DIOMEDES | Fair Lady Cressid,<br>So please you, save the thanks this prince expects.<br>The lustre in your eye, heaven in your cheek,<br>Pleads your fair usage; and to Diomed<br>You shall be mistress, and command him wholly. |
